Number of rollouts per task\\n\\n    #################################################################################################################\\n    # Utils\\n    #################################################################################################################\\n    video_out_path: str = \\\"data/libero/videos\\\"  # longest training demo has 270 steps\\n    elif args.task_suite_name == \\\"libero_10\\\":\\n        max_steps = 520  # longest training demo has 505 steps\\n    elif args.task_suite_name == \\\"libero_90\\\":\\n        max_steps = 400  # longest training demo has 373 steps\\n    else:\\n        raise ValueError(f\\\"Unknown task suite: {args.task_suite_name}\\\")\\n\\n    client = _websocket_client_policy.WebsocketClientPolicy(args.host, args.port)\\n\\n    # Start evaluation\\n    total_episodes, total_successes = 0, 0\\n    for task_id in tqdm.tqdm(range(num_tasks_in_suite)):\\n        # Get task\\n        task = task_suite.get_task(task_id)\\n\\n        # Reset environment\\n            env.reset()\\n            action_plan = collections.deque()\\n\\n            # Set initial states\\n            obs = env.set_init_state(initial_states[episode_idx])\\n\\n            # Setup\\n            t = 0\\n            replay_images = []\\n\\n            logging.info(f\\\"Starting episode {task_episodes+1}...\\\")\\n            while t < max\\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 1194 字符] ...\\n\\n         if not action_plan:\\n                        # Finished executing previous action chunk -- compute new chunk\\n                        # Prepare observations dict\\n                        element = {\\n                            \\\"observation/image\\\": img,\\n                            \\\"observation/wrist_image\\\": wrist_img,\\n                            \\\"observation/state\\\": np.concatenate(\\n                                (\\n                                    obs[\\\"robot0_eef_pos\\\"],\\n                                    _quat2axisangle(obs[\\\"robot0_eef_quat\\\"]),\\n                                    obs[\\\"robot0_gripper_qpos\\\"],\\n                                )\\n                            ),\\n                            \\\"prompt\\\": str(task_description),\\n                        }\\n\\n                        # Query model to get action\\n                        action_chunk = client.infer(element)[\\\"actions\\\"]\\n                        assert (\\n                            len(action_chunk) >= args.replan_steps\\n                        ), f\\\"We want to replan every {args.replan_steps} steps, but policy only predicts {len(action_chunk)} steps.\\\"\\n                        action_plan.extend(action_chunk[: args.replan_steps])\\n\\n                    action = action_plan.popleft()\\n\\n                    # Log current results\\n            logging.info(f\\\"Success: {done}\\\")\\n            logging.info(f\\\"# episodes completed so far: {total_episodes}\\\")\\n            logging.info(f\\\"# successes: {total_successes} ({total_successes / total_episodes * 100:.1f}%)\\\")\\n\\n        # Log final results\\n        logging.info(f\\\"Current task success rate: {float(task_successes) / float(task_episodes)}\\\")\\n        logging.info(f\\\"Current total success rate: {float(total_successes) / float(total_episodes)}\\\")\\n\\n    logging.info(f\\\"Total success rate: {float(total_successes) / float(total_episodes)}\\\")\\n    logging.info(f\\\"Total episodes: {total_episodes}\\\")\\n\\n\\ndef _get_libero_env(task, resolution, seed):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Initializes and returns the LIBERO environment, along with the task description.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    task_description = task.language\\n    task_bddl_file = pathlib.Path(get_libero_path(\\\"bddl_files\\\")) / task.problem_folder / task.bddl_file\\n    env_args = {\\\"bddl_file_name\\\": task_bddl_file, \\\"camera_heights\\\": resolution, \\\"camera_widths\\\": resolution}\\n    env = OffScreenRenderEnv(**env_args)\\n    env.seed(seed)  # clip quaternion\\n    if quat[3] > 1.0:\\n        quat[3] = 1.0\\n    elif quat[3] < -1.0:\\n        quat[3] = -1.0\\n\\n    den = np.sqrt(1.0 - quat[3] * quat[3])\\n    if math.isclose(den, 0.0):\\n        # Let me analyze the three eval approaches:\n\n**openpi LIBERO example (`vlsa-aegis/openpi/examples/libero/main.py`)**:\n- Uses `WebsocketClientPolicy` to infer\n- `action_chunk = client.infer(element)[\"actions\"]`\n- Action is sent directly to `env.step(action.tolist())` - **no denormalization needed** because the policy server handles denormalization internally\n\n**AEGIS eval (`vlsa-aegis/main/main_aegis.py`)**:\n- Also uses `WebsocketClientPolicy` to infer\n\n\n- `action_chunk = client.infer(element)[\"actions\"]`\n- Similarly sends action to env - **no denormalization needed**\n\n**Our eval script (`scripts/eval_safelibero.py`)** - I need to check how we're handling actions. calls `policy.infer()` - which likely skips that denormalization step entirely.\n\nI need to examine the `evaluate_task()` function to see how actions are processed, check whether `policy.infer()` has built-in denormalization, and look at the normalization statistics we're \\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\nEvaluate a VLA model on SafeLIBERO benchmark.\\\\nSupports three model types:\\\\n  - baseline: Standard openpi pi0 LoRA fine-tune\\\\n  - a1: pi0 + SDF decoder + EE extractor (auxiliary task; inference identical to baseline)\\\\n  - a3: pi0 + SDF decoder + EE extractor + SDF Gradient Refiner (inference-time refinement)\\\\n\\\\nMeasures: Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR), Task Success Rate (TSR), trajectory smoothness.\\\\n\\\\nUsage:\\\\n    # Evaluate baseline\\\\n    python scripts/eval_safelibero.py \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --checkpoint /root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/baseline_spatial_bowl_0 \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --config_name baseline_spatial_bowl \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --suite safelibero_spatial \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --task_id 0 \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --safety_level II \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --n_episodes 50\\\\n\\\\n    # Evaluate A1 (same inference as baseline, loads SDF modules for analysis)\\\\n    python scripts/eval_safelibero.py \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --model_type a1 \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --checkpoint /root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/a1_sdf_spatial_bowl/a1_sdf_spatial_bowl_seed0/1000 \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --config_name baseline_spatial_bowl \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --suite safelibero_spatial \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --task_id 0 \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --n_episodes 50\\\\n\\\\n    # Evaluate A3 (inference-time SDF gradient refinement)\\\\n    python scripts/eval_safelibero.py \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --model_type a3 \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --checkpoint /root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/a3_grad_refine_spatial_bowl/... agentview_image is None or wrist_image is None:\\n        for key in obs:\\n            if \\\"agentview\\\" in key and \\\"image\\\" in key:\\n                agentview_image = obs[key]\\n            if \\\"eye_in_hand\\\" in key and \\\"image\\\" in key:\\n                wrist_image = obs[key]\\n\\n    # State must match training data format: [ee_pos(3), ee_ori_rotvec(3), gripper_states(2)]\\n robot0_eef_quat (quaternion) which we convert to rotation vector.\\n    ee_pos = obs.get(\\\"robot0_eef_pos\\\", np.zeros(3))\\n    ee_quat = obs.get(\\\"robot0_eef_quat\\\", np.array([1.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0]))\\n    gripper_qpos = obs.get(\\\"robot0_gripper_qpos\\\", np.zeros(2))\\n\\n    # robosuite uses [x, y, z, w] quaternion convention, same as scipy\\n    ee_ori = _Rotation.from_quat(ee_quat).as_rotvec()\\n\\n    state = np.concatenate([ee_pos, ee_ori, gripper_qpos]).astype(np.float32)\\n\\n    policy_input = {\\n        \\\"observation/image\\\": agentview_image,\\n        \\\"observation/wrist_image\\\": wrist_image,\\n        \\\"observation/state\\\": state,\\n        \\\"prompt\\\": language_instruction,\\n    }\\n\\n    result = policy.infer(policy_input)\\n    actions = result[\\\"actions\\\"]\\n\\n    if len(actions.shape) == 2:\\n        return actions[0]\\n    return actions\\n\\n\\n# {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_013t2pEEoB8sowtbD1YLFtkJ","type":"tool_result","content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\nEvaluate a VLA model on SafeLIBERO benchmark.\\\\nSupports three model types:\\\\n  - baseline: Standard openpi pi0 LoRA fine-tune\\\\n  - a1: pi0 + SDF decoder + EE extractor (auxiliary task; inference identical to baseline)\\\\n  - a3: pi0 + SDF decoder + EE extractor + SDF Gradient Refiner (inference-time refinement)\\\\n\\\\nMeasures: Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR), Task Success Rate (TSR), trajectory smoothness.\\\\n\\\\nUsage:\\\\n    # Evaluate baseline\\\\n    python scripts/eval_safelibero.py \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --checkpoint /root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/baseline_spatial_bowl_0 \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --config_name baseline_spatial_bowl \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --suite safelibero_spatial \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --task_id 0 \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --safety_level II \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --n_episodes 50\\\\n\\\\n    # Evaluate A1 (same inference as baseline, loads SDF modules for analysis)\\\\n    python scripts/eval_safelibero.py \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --model_type a1 \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --checkpoint /root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/a1_sdf_spatial_bowl/a1_sdf_spatial_bowl_seed0/1000 \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --config_name baseline_spatial_bowl \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --suite safelibero_spatial \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --task_id 0 \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --n_episodes 50\\\\n\\\\n    # Evaluate A3 (inference-time SDF gradient refinement)\\\\n    python scripts/eval_safelibero.py \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --model_type a3 \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --checkpoint /root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/a3_grad_refine_spatial_bowl/... \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --config_name baseline_spatial_bowl \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --sdf_gt_path /root/colliforce-vla/data/sdf_gt_spatial_bowl.npz \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --suite safelibero_spatial \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --task_id 0 \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --refine_alpha 0.1 \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --refine_margin 0.01 \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --n_episodes 50\\\\n\\\\n    # Evaluate with default openpi LIBERO policy\\\\n    python scripts/eval_safelibero.py \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --default_policy \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --suite safelibero_object \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --task_id 1 \\\\\\\\\\\\n        --n_episodes 10\\\\n\\\\n    # Ignored for PyTorch models.\\\\n            transforms: Input data transformations to apply before inference.\\\\n            output_transforms: Output data transformations to apply after inference.\\\\n            sample_kwargs: Additional keyword arguments to pass to model.sample_actions.\\\\n            metadata: Additional metadata to store with the policy.\\\\n            pytorch_device: Device to use for PyTorch models (e.g., \\\\\\\"cpu\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"cuda:0\\\\\\\").\\\\n                          Only relevant when is_pytorch=True.\\\\n            is_pytorch: Whether the model is a PyTorch model. If False, assumes JAX model.\\\\n        \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n        self._model = model\\\\n        self._input_transform = _transforms.compose(transforms)\\\\n        self._output_transform = _transforms.compose(output_transforms)\\\\n        self._sample_kwargs = sample_kwargs or {}\\\\n        self._metadata = metadata or {}\\\\n        self._is_pytorch_model = is_pytorch\\\\n        self._pytorch_device = pytorch_device\\\\n\\\\n        if self._is_pytorch_model:\\\\n            self._model = self._model.to(pytorch_device)\\\\n            self._model.eval()\\\\n            self._sample_actions = model.sample_actions\\\\n        else:\\\\n            # JAX model setup\\\\n            self._sample_actions = nnx_utils.module_jit(model.sample_actions)\\\\n            self._rng = rng or jax.random.key(0)\\\\n\\\\n    @override\\\\n    def infer(self, obs: dict, *, noise: np.ndarray | None = None) -> dict:  # type: ignore[misc]\\\\n        # Make a copy since transformations may modify the inputs in place.\\\\n        inputs = jax.tree.map(lambda x: x, obs)\\\\n        inputs = self._input_transform(inputs)\\\\n        if not self._is_pytorch_model:\\\\n            # Make a batch and convert to jax.Array.\\\\n            inputs = jax.tree.map(lambda x: jnp.asarray(x)[np.newaxis, ...], inputs)\\\\n            self._rng, sample_rng_or_pytorch_device = jax.random.split(self._rng)\\\\n        else:\\\\n            # Convert inputs to PyTorch tensors and move to correct device\\\\n            inputs = jax.tree.map(lambda x: torch.from_numpy(np.array(x)).to(self._pytorch_device)[None, ...], inputs)\\\\n            sample_rng_or_pytorch_device = self._pytorch_device\\\\n\\\\n        # Prepare kwargs for sample_actions\\\\n        sample_kwargs = dict(self._sample_kwargs)\\\\n        if noise is not None:\\\\n            noise = torch.from_numpy(noise).to(self._pytorch_device) if self._is_pytorch_model else jnp.asarray(noise)\\\\n\\\\n            if noise.ndim == 2:  # If noise is (action_horizon, action_dim), add batch dimension\\\\n                noise = noise[None, ...]  # Make it (1, action_horizon, action_dim)\\\\n            sample_kwargs[\\\\\\\"noise\\\\\\\"] = noise\\\\n\\\\n        observation = _model.Observation.from_dict(inputs)\\\\n        start_time = time.monotonic()\\\\n        outputs = {\\\\n            \\\\\\\"state\\\\\\\": inputs[\\\\\\\"state\\\\\\\"],\\\\n            \\\\\\\"actions\\\\\\\": self._sample_actions(sample_rng_or_pytorch_device, observation, **sample_kwargs),\\\\n        }\\\\n        model_time = time.monotonic() - start_time\\\\n        if self._is_pytorch_model:\\\\n            outputs = jax.tree.map(lambda x: np.asarray(x[0, ...].detach().cpu()), outputs)\\\\n        else:\\\\n            outputs = jax.tree.map(lambda x: np.asarray(x[0, ...]), outputs)\\\\n\\\\n        outputs = self._output_transform(outputs)\\\\n        outputs[\\\\\\\"policy_timing\\\\\\\"] = {\\\\n            \\\\\\\"infer_ms\\\\\\\": model_time * 1000,\\\\n        }\\\\n        return outputs\\\\n\\\\n    @property\\\\n    def metadata(self) -> If noise is (action_horizon, action_dim), add batch dimension\\n                noise = noise[None, ...]  # Make it (1, action_horizon, action_dim)\\n            sample_kwargs[\\\"noise\\\"] = noise\\n\\n        observation = _model.Observation.from_dict(inputs)\\n        start_time = time.monotonic()\\n        outputs = {\\n            \\\"state\\\": inputs[\\\"state\\\"],\\n            \\\"actions\\\": self._sample_actions(sample_rng_or_pytorch_device, observation, **sample_kwargs),\\n        }\\n        model_time = time.monotonic() - start_time\\n        if self._is_pytorch_model:\\n            outputs = jax.tree.map(lambda x: np.asarray(x[0, ...].detach().cpu()), outputs)\\n        else:\\n            outputs = jax.tree.map(lambda x: np.asarray(x[0, ...]), outputs)\\n\\n        outputs = self._output_transform(outputs)\\n        outputs[\\\"policy_timing\\\"] = {\\n            \\\"infer_ms\\\": model_time * 1000,\\n        }\\n        return outputs\\n\\n    @property\\n    def metadata(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\\n        return self._metadata\\n\\n\\nclass PolicyRecorder(_base_policy.BasePolicy):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Records the policy's behavior to disk.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(self, policy: _base_policy.BasePolicy, record_dir: str):\\n        self._policy = policy\\n\\n        logging.info(f\\\"Dumping policy records to: {record_dir}\\\")\\n        self._record_dir = pathlib.Path(record_dir)\\n        self._record_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\\n        self._record_step = 0\\n\\n    @override\\n    def infer(self, obs: dict) -> dict:  #        The function automatically detects whether the model is PyTorch-based by checking for the\\\\n        presence of \\\\\\\"model.safensors\\\\\\\" in the checkpoint directory.\\\\n model.paligemma_with_expert.to_bfloat16_for_selected_params(\\\\\\\"bfloat16\\\\\\\")\\\\n    else:\\\\n        model = train_config.model.load(_model.restore_params(checkpoint_dir / \\\\\\\"params\\\\\\\", dtype=jnp.bfloat16))\\\\n    data_config = train_config.data.create(train_config.assets_dirs, train_config.model)\\\\n    if norm_stats is None:\\\\n        # We are loading the norm stats from the checkpoint instead of the config assets dir to make sure\\\\n        # that the policy is using the same normalization stats as the original training process.\\\\n        if data_config.asset_id is None:\\\\n            raise ValueError(\\\\\\\"Asset id is required to load norm stats.\\\\\\\")\\\\n        norm_stats = _checkpoints.load_norm_stats(checkpoint_dir / \\\\\\\"cpu\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\n    return _policy.Policy(\\\\n        model,\\\\n        transforms=[\\\\n            *repack_transforms.inputs,\\\\n {\"stdout\": \"import logging\\nimport os\\nimport pathlib\\nfrom typing import Any\\n\\nimport jax.numpy as jnp\\n\\nimport openpi.models.model as _model\\nimport openpi.policies.policy as _policy\\nimport openpi.shared.download as download\\nfrom openpi.training import checkpoints as _checkpoints\\nfrom openpi.training import config as _config\\nimport openpi.transforms as transforms\\n\\n\\ndef create_trained_policy(\\n    train_config: _config.TrainConfig,\\n    checkpoint_dir: pathlib.Path | str,\\n    *,\\n    repack_transforms: transforms.Group | None = None,\\n    sample_kwargs: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\\n    default_prompt: str | None = None,\\n    norm_stats: dict[str, transforms.NormStats] | None = None,\\n    pytorch_device: str | None = None,\\n) -> _policy.Policy:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Create a policy from a trained checkpoint.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        train_config: The training config to use to create the model.\\n        checkpoint_dir: The directory to load the model from.\\n        repack_transforms: Optional transforms that will be applied before any other transforms.\\n        sample_kwargs: The kwargs to pass to the `sample_actions` method. If not provided, the default\\n            kwargs will be used.\\n        default_prompt: The default prompt to use for the policy. Will inject the prompt into the input\\n            data if it doesn't already exist.\\n        norm_stats: The norm stats to use for the policy. If not provided, the norm stats will be loaded\\n            from the checkpoint directory.\\n        pytorch_device: Device to use for PyTorch models (e.g., \\\"cpu\\\", \\\"cuda\\\", \\\"cuda:0\\\").\\n                      If None and is_pytorch=True, will use \\\"cuda\\\" if available, otherwise \\\"cpu\\\".\\n\\n    Note:\\n        The function automatically detects whether the model is PyTorch-based by checking for the\\n        presence of \\\"model.safensors\\\" in the checkpoint directory.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    repack_transforms = repack_transforms or transforms.Group()\\n    checkpoint_dir = download.maybe_download(str(checkpoint_dir))\\n\\n    # Check if this is a PyTorch model by looking for model.safetensors\\n    weight_path = os.path.join(checkpoint_dir, \\\"model.safetensors\\\")\\n    is_pytorch = os.path.exists(weight_path)\\n\\n    logging.info(\\\"Loading model...\\\")\\n    if is_pytorch:\\n        model = train_config.model.load_pytorch(train_config, weight_path)\\n        model.paligemma_with_expert.to_bfloat16_for_selected_params(\\\"bfloat16\\\")\\n    else:\\n        model = train_config.model.load(_model.restore_params(checkpoint_dir / \\\"params\\\", dtype=jnp.bfloat16))\\n    data_config = train_config.data.create(train_config.assets_dirs, train_config.model)\\n    if norm_stats is None:\\n        # We are loading the norm stats from the checkpoint instead of the config assets dir to make sure\\n        # that the policy is using the same normalization stats as the original training process.\\n        if data_config.asset_id is None:\\n            raise ValueError(\\\"Asset id is required to load norm stats.\\\")\\n        norm_stats = _checkpoints.load_norm_stats(checkpoint_dir / \\\"assets\\\", data_config.asset_id)\\n\\n    # Determine the device to use for PyTorch models\\n    if is_pytorch and pytorch_device is None:\\n        try:\\n            import torch\\n\\n            pytorch_device = \\\"cuda\\\" if torch.cuda.is_available() else \\\"cpu\\\"\\n        except ImportError:\\n            pytorch_device = \\\"cpu\\\"\\n\\n    return _policy.Policy(\\n        model,\\n        transforms=[\\n            *repack_transforms.inputs,\\n            transforms.InjectDefaultPrompt(default_prompt),\\n            *data_config.data_transforms.inputs,\\n            transforms.Normalize(norm_stats, use_quantiles=data_config.use_quantile_norm),\\n            *data_config.model_transforms.inputs,\\n        ],\\n        output_transforms=[\\n            *data_config.model_transforms.outputs,\\n            transforms.Unnormalize(norm_stats, use_quantiles=data_config.use_quantile_norm),\\n            *data_config.data_transforms.outputs,\\n            *repack_transforms.outputs,\\n        ],\\n        sample_kwargs=sample_kwargs,\\n        metadata=train_config.policy_metadata,\\n        is_pytorch=is_pytorch,\\n        pytorch_device=pytorch_device if is_pytorch else None,\\n    )\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.48}}"}}}

Normalize inputs (including state)\n2. Run model\n3. Unnormalize outputs (including actions)\n\nSo **denormalization IS happening** in the `policy.infer()` call. This means the output actions should be properly denormalized.\n\nLet me now look at what happens with the action AFTER `policy.infer()` returns it:\n\n\nIn the eval function, I'm extracting the actions from the policy output and handling the batch dimension if needed, then passing that action directly to the environment step. But the eval loop itself was cut off, so I need to check how AEGIS actually processes the action after retrieving it from the policy.\n\nLooking at the AEGIS implementation, they're collecting multiple action chunks from the model and maintaining a queue, popping one action at a time to step the environment. In contrast, our eval seems to call the policy at every step and take just the first action. The critical difference is that AEGIS batches actions and replans every few steps, while our approach regenerates the action at each timestep—this could significantly impact performance and efficiency.\n\nOn the state representation front, I'm noticing a potential mismatch: we're using rotation vectors from `Rotation.from_quat().as_rotvec()` while AEGIS uses axis-angle conversion, and though these should theoretically be equivalent, I need to verify they're actually producing the same values in practice. I need to trace through the eval loop more carefully to see what happens after the policy generates an action—there might be clipping, reshaping, or some other modification happening between inference and the environment step. Let me also double-check the action extraction logic in `get_action_from_policy` to confirm it's correctly pulling the first timestep from the action horizon and that the resulting shape matches what the environment expects.\n\nLooking at both the standard eval and the AEGIS example, they both extract a 7-dimensional action (3D delta position, 3D rotation, 1D gripper), but the AEGIS code applies additional processing like Control Barrier Functions and obstacle detection that might be modifying or constraining the action before it reaches the environment.\n\nThe real issue is probably one of three things: the model might be getting called every step while only using the first action instead of maintaining a temporal action sequence, there could be a normalization mismatch between how Assumes that '/' was used as a separator.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    return traverse_util.unflatten_dict(tree, sep=\\\"/\\\")\\n\\n\\ndef transform_dict(patterns: Mapping[str, str | None], tree: at.PyTree) -> at.PyTree:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Transform the structure of a nested dictionary using a set of patterns.\\n\\n    The transformation is defined using the `patterns` dictionary. The keys are the\\n    input keys that should be matched and the values are the new names inside the output\\n    dictionary. If the value is None, the input key is removed.\\n\\n    Both keys and values should represent flattened paths using '/' as the separator.\\n    Keys can be regular expressions and values can include backreferences to the\\n    matched groups (see `re.sub` for more details). Note that the regular expression\\n    must match the entire key.\\n\\n    The order inside the `patterns` dictionary is important. Only the first pattern that\\n    matches the input key will be used.\\n\\n    See unit tests for more examples.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        patterns: A mapping from old keys to new keys.\\n        tree: The nested dictionary to transform.\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        The transformed nested dictionary.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    data = flatten_dict(tree)\\n\\n    # Compile the patterns.\\n    compiled = {re.compile(k): v for k, v in patterns.items()}\\n\\n    output = {}\\n    for k in data:\\n        for pattern, repl in compiled.items():\\n            if pattern.fullmatch(k):\\n                new_k = pattern.sub(repl, k, count=1) if repl is not None else None\\n                break\\n        else:\\n            # Use the original key if no match is found.\\n            new_k = k\\n\\n        if new_k is not None:\\n            if new_k in output:\\n                raise ValueError(f\\\"Key '{new_k}' already exists in output\\\")\\n            output[new_k] = data[k]\\n\\n    # Validate the output structure to make sure that it can be unflattened.\\n    names = sorted(output)\\n    for i in range(len(names) - If the model outputs delta actions and we're not converting them back to absolute, or if the model outputs absolute actions and the env expects delta actions...\n\nActually wait, let me think. The LIBERO env uses OSC (Operational Space Control) and expects **delta actions** (delta position + delta orientation + gripper). The model outputs normalized delta actions, they get converted to absolute coordinates, and then we're feeding absolute actions to an environment that expects deltas—that's the bug.\n\nOr maybe the training data is already in absolute space and the model learns absolute actions, which then need to be converted to delta for the environment. Just make sure it matches the model config\\n        # you chose above.\\n        freeze_filter=pi0_config.Pi0Config(\\n            paligemma_variant=\\\"gemma_2b_lora\\\", action_expert_variant=\\\"gemma_300m_lora\\\"\\n        ).get_freeze_filter(),\\n        # Turn off EMA for LoRA finetuning.\\n        ema_decay=None,\\n    ),\\n    TrainConfig(\\n        name=\\\"pi0_fast_libero\\\",\\n        # Here is an example of loading a pi0-FAST model for full finetuning.\\n        # Modify action_dim and action_horizon to match your dataset (action horizon is equal to\\n        # the desired action chunk length).\\n        # The max_token_len is the maximum number of (non-image) tokens the model can handle.\\n        # This includes the tokenized prompt, proprioceptive state, and (FAST-tokenized) action tokens.\\n        # Choosing this value too small may chop off tokens at the end of your sequence (the code will throw\\n        # a warning), while choosing it too large will waste memory (since we pad each batch element to the\\n        # max_token_len). {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014xvjiYpbzJW274s2xtau4U","type":"tool_result","content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"class LeRobotLiberoDataConfig(DataConfigFactory):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    This config is used to configure transforms that are applied at various parts of the data pipeline.\\\\n    For your own dataset, you can copy this class and modify the transforms to match your dataset based on the\\\\n    comments below.\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\n    extra_delta_transform: bool = False\\\\n\\\\n    @override\\\\n    def create(self, assets_dirs: pathlib.Path, model_config: _model.BaseModelConfig) -> DataConfig:\\\\n        # The repack transform is *only* applied to the data coming from the dataset,\\\\n        # and *not* during inference. We can use it to make inputs from the dataset look\\\\n        # as close as possible to those coming from the inference environment (e.g. match the keys).\\\\n        # Below, we match the keys in the dataset (which we defined in the data conversion script) to\\\\n        # the keys we use in our inference pipeline (defined in the inference script for libero).\\\\n        # For your own dataset, first figure out what keys your environment passes to the policy server\\\\n        # and then modify the mappings below so your dataset's keys get matched to those target keys.\\\\n        # The repack transform simply remaps key names here.\\\\n        repack_transform = _transforms.Group(\\\\n            inputs=[\\\\n                _transforms.RepackTransform(\\\\n                    {\\\\n                        \\\\\\\"observation/image\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"image\\\\\\\",\\\\n                        \\\\\\\"observation/wrist_image\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"wrist_image\\\\\\\",\\\\n                        \\\\\\\"observation/state\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"state\\\\\\\",\\\\n                        \\\\\\\"actions\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"actions\\\\\\\",\\\\n                        \\\\\\\"prompt\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"prompt\\\\\\\",\\\\n                    }\\\\n                )\\\\n            ]\\\\n        )\\\\n\\\\n        # The data transforms are applied to the data coming from the dataset *and* during inference.\\\\n        # Below, we define the transforms for data going into the model (``inputs``) and the transforms\\\\n        # for data coming out of the model (``outputs``) (the latter is only used during inference).\\\\n        # We defined these transforms in `libero_policy.py`. You can check the detailed comments there for\\\\n        # how to modify the transforms to match your dataset. Once you created your own transforms, you can\\\\n        # replace the transforms below with your own.\\\\n        data_transforms = _transforms.Group(\\\\n            inputs=[libero_policy.LiberoInputs(model_type=model_config.model_type)],\\\\n            outputs=[libero_policy.LiberoOutputs()],\\\\n        )\\\\n\\\\n        # One additional data transform: pi0 models are trained on delta actions (relative to the first\\\\n        # state in each action chunk). IF your data has ``absolute`` actions (e.g. target joint angles)\\\\n        # you can uncomment the following line to convert the actions to delta actions. The only exception\\\\n        # is for the gripper actions which are always absolute.\\\\n        # In the example below, we would apply the delta conversion to the first 6 actions (joints) and\\\\n        # leave the 7th action (gripper) unchanged, i.e. absolute.\\\\n        # In Libero, the raw actions in the dataset are already delta actions, so we *do not* need to\\\\n        # apply a separate delta conversion (that's why it's commented out). Choose whether to apply this\\\\n        # transform based on whether your dataset uses ``absolute`` or ``delta`` actions out of the box.\\\\n\\\\n        # LIBERO already represents actions as deltas, but we have some old Pi0 checkpoints that are trained with this\\\\n        # extra delta transform.\\\\n        if self.extra_delta_transform:\\\\n            delta_action_mask = _transforms.make_bool_mask(6, -1)\\\\n            data_transforms = data_transforms.push(\\\\n                inputs=[_transforms.DeltaActions(delta_action_mask)],\\\\n                outputs=[_transforms.AbsoluteActions(delta_action_mask)],\\\\n            )\\\\n\\\\n        # Model transforms include things like tokenizing the prompt and action targets\\\\n        # You do not need to change anything here for your own dataset.\\\\n        model_transforms = ModelTransformFactory()(model_config)\\\\n\\\\n        \\\\n\\\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 24130 字符] ...\\\\n\\\\n5e-5,\\\\n            decay_steps=1_000_000,\\\\n            decay_lr=5e-5,\\\\n        ),\\\\n        num_train_steps=100_000,\\\\n        batch_size=256,\\\\n        log_interval=100,\\\\n        save_interval=5000,\\\\n        keep_period=10_000,\\\\n        num_workers=0,  # Important: RLDS DataLoader requires num_workers=0, handles multi-processing internally\\\\n    ),\\\\n    TrainConfig(\\\\n        # This config is for fine-tuning pi05-DROID on a custom (smaller) DROID dataset.\\\\n        # Here, we use LeRobot data format (like for all other fine-tuning examples)\\\\n        # To convert your custom DROID dataset (<10s of hours) to LeRobot format, see examples/droid/convert_droid_data_to_lerobot.py\\\\n        name=\\\\\\\"pi05_droid_finetune\\\\\\\",\\\\n        model=pi0_config.Pi0Config(\\\\n            pi05=True,\\\\n            action_dim=32,  # pi05 is trained with 32-dim actions\\\\n            action_horizon=16,\\\\n        ),\\\\n        data=LeRobotDROIDDataConfig(\\\\n            # Replace with your custom DROID LeRobot dataset repo id.\\\\n            repo_id=\\\\\\\"your_hf_username/my_droid_dataset\\\\\\\",\\\\n            base_config=DataConfig(prompt_from_task=True),\\\\n            assets=AssetsConfig(\\\\n                # Important: reuse the original DROID norm stats during fine-tuning!\\\\n                assets_dir=\\\\\\\"gs://openpi-assets/checkpoints/pi05_droid/assets\\\\\\\",\\\\n                asset_id=\\\\\\\"droid\\\\\\\",\\\\n            ),\\\\n        ),\\\\n        weight_loader=weight_loaders.CheckpointWeightLoader(\\\\\\\"gs://openpi-assets/checkpoints/pi05_droid/params\\\\\\\"),\\\\n        num_train_steps=20_000,\\\\n        batch_size=32,\\\\n    ),\\\\n    #\\\\n    # ALOHA Sim configs. Let me trace through the `extra_delta_transform=True` logic:\n\n**Comment in the code says:**\n> \"In Libero, the raw actions in the dataset are already delta actions, so we *do not* need to apply a separate delta conversion (that's why it's commented out). Choose whether to apply this transform based on whether your dataset uses ``absolute`` or ``delta`` actions out of the box.\"\n> \n> \"LIBERO already represents actions as deltas, but we have some old Pi0 checkpoints that are trained with this extra delta transform.\"\n\nSo when `extra_delta_transform=True`:\n\n\n- **Training input transform**: `DeltaActions(mask)` subtracts state from the actions, which double-deltas the already-delta Libero data: `action = delta_action - state`. back to the original `delta_action` by computing `output = model_output + state`.\n\nThis creates a complete pipeline where training and inference transforms are inverses of each other.\n\nNow tracing through the full inference flow in `policy.infer()`: the input transform first converts the observation format and normalizes the state, then the model generates action predictions, and finally the *self.outputs))\\\\n\\\\n\\\\n@dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True)\\\\nclass CompositeTransform(DataTransformFn):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"A composite transform that applies a sequence of {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01GkR7Pf57F7m3GJANJzaK5T","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"No `safelibero` in the training config file. So the baseline was trained with a custom config or a modified version that uses `safelibero_object` as the asset_id.\n\nThe `load_norm_stats` function:\n```python\ndef load_norm_stats(assets_dir, asset_id):\n    norm_stats_dir = epath.Path(assets_dir) / asset_id\n    norm_stats = _normalize.load(norm_stats_dir)\n    return norm_stats\n```\n\nSo when we call `create_trained_policy(config, checkpoint_dir)`:\n\n\nThe data config gets created with asset_id \"physical-intelligence/libero\" from the config, then we try to load norm stats from that path in the checkpoint assets directory. \\\"..\\\"))\\n\\nos.environ[\\\"HF_LEROBOT_HOME\\\"] = \\\"/root/autodl-tmp/colliforce_data\\\"\\n\\nimport openpi.models.pi0_config as pi0_config\\nimport openpi.training.config as _config\\nfrom openpi.training.optimizer import CosineDecaySchedule\\nfrom scripts.train_pytorch import TrainHooks, train_loop\\nfrom colliforce.lora_utils import inject_lora_pi0\\n\\n\\nSCENE_CONFIG = {\\n    \\\"spatial_bowl\\\": {\\n        \\\"description\\\": \\\"SafeLIBERO Spatial: bowl between plate and ramekin (Level II)\\\",\\n        \\\"data_subdir\\\": \\\"safelibero_spatial\\\",\\n    },\\n    \\\"object_pudding\\\": {\\n        \\\"description\\\": \\\"SafeLIBERO Object: chocolate pudding to basket (Level I)\\\",\\n        \\\"data_subdir\\\": \\\"safelibero_object\\\",\\n    },\\n}\\n\\nCHECKPOINT_BASE = \\\"/root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints\\\"\\nDATA_BASE = \\\"/root/autodl-tmp/colliforce_data\\\"\\nWEIGHTS_BASE = \\\"/root/autodl-tmp/openpi_weights\\\"\\n\\n\\ndef parse_args():\\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=\\\"Baseline pi0 fine-tune\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--scene\\\", type=str, required=True, choices=list(SCENE_CONFIG.keys()))\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--seed\\\", type=int, default=0)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--batch_size\\\", type=int, default=32)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--num_train_steps\\\", type=int, default=30000)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--lr\\\", type=float, default=2.5e-5)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--save_interval\\\", type=int, default=2000)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--log_interval\\\", type=int, default=100)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--exp_name\\\", type=str, default=None)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--dry_run\\\", action=\\\"store_true\\\", help=\\\"Quick 2-step test without wandb\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--resume\\\", action=\\\"store_true\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--data_dir\\\", type=str, default=None, help=\\\"Override data directory\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--weight_path\\\", type=str, default=None, help=\\\"Override base weight path\\\")\\n    return parser.parse_args()\\n\\n\\nclass BaselineLoRAHooks(TrainHooks):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Inject manual LoRA and freeze non-LoRA params for fair comparison with A1/A3.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def on_model_created(self, model, device):\\n        _m = model.module if hasattr(model, \\\"module\\\") else model\\n\\n        # Inject LoRA: PaliGemma rank=16, Action Expert rank=32\\n        self.lora_params, self._pali_lora_params, self._expert_lora_params = inject_lora_pi0(\\n            _m, pali_rank=16, pali_alpha=16.0, expert_rank=32, expert_alpha=32.0)\\n\\n        # Freeze all non-LoRA params\\n        for name, p in _m.named_parameters():\\n            if \\\"lora\\\" not in name.lower():\\n                p.requires_grad = False\\n\\n        n_lora = sum(p.numel() for p in self.lora_params)\\n        n_total = sum(p.numel() for p in _m.parameters())\\n        n_trainable = sum(p.numel() for p in _m.parameters() if p.requires_grad)\\n        logging.info(\\\"=== Baseline LoRA Hooks ===\\\")\\n        logging.info(\\\"  Total params: %.2fM\\\", n_total / 1e6)\\n        logging.info(\\\"  LoRA params: %.2fM (PaliGemma: %.2fM, Expert: %.2fM)\\\",\\n                     n_lora / 1e6,\\n                     sum(p.numel() for p in self._pali_lora_params) / 1e6,\\n                     sum(p.numel() for p in self._expert_lora_params) / 1e6)\\n        logging.info(\\\"  Trainable params: %.2fM\\\", n_trainable / 1e6)\\n\\n    def extra_params(self):\\n        return []\\n\\n    def on_step_start(self, global_step, model):\\n        if global_step < 100 and global_step % 10 == 0:\\n            _m = model.module if hasattr(model, \\\"module\\\") else model\\n            trainable = {n: p.numel() for n, p in _m.named_parameters() if p.requires_grad}\\n            print(f\\\"[Step {global_step}] Trainable params: {sum(trainable.values()):,}\\\")\\n            print(f\\\"[Step {global_step}] LoRA params: {sum(v for n, v in trainable.items() if 'lora' in n):,}\\\")\\n\\n\\ndef build_config(args) -> _config.TrainConfig:\\n    scene_cfg = SCENE_CONFIG[args.scene]\\n    data_dir = args.data_dir or os.path.join(DATA_BASE, scene_cfg[\\\"data_subdir\\\"])\\n    weight_path = args.weight_path or os.path.join(WEIGHTS_BASE, \\\"pi0_base\\\")\\n    exp_name = args.exp_name or f\\\"baseline_{args.scene}_seed{args.seed}\\\"\\n\\n    num_steps = 2 if args.dry_run else args.num_train_steps\\n\\n    model_config = pi0_config.Pi0Config(\\n        paligemma_variant=\\\"gemma_2b\\\",\\n        action_expert_variant=\\\"gemma_300m\\\",\\n    )\\n\\n    config = _config.TrainConfig(\\n        name=f\\\"baseline_{args.scene}\\\",\\n        project_name=\\\"colliforce-vla\\\",\\n        exp_name=exp_name,\\n        model=model_config,\\n        data=_config.LeRobotLiberoDataConfig(\\n            repo_id=scene_cfg[\\\"data_subdir\\\"],\\n            base_config=_config.DataConfig(prompt_from_task=True),\\n            extra_delta_transform=False,\\n        ),\\n        pytorch_weight_path=weight_path,\\n        ema_decay=None,\\n        lr_schedule=CosineDecaySchedule(\\n            warmup_steps=min(1000, num_steps // 3),\\n            peak_lr=args.lr,\\n            decay_steps=num_steps,\\n            decay_lr=args.lr / 10,\\n        ),\\n        num_train_steps=num_steps,\\n        batch_size=args.batch_size,\\n        save_interval=args.save_interval if not args.dry_run else num_steps,\\n        log_interval=args.log_interval if not args.dry_run else 1,\\n        checkpoint_base_dir=CHECKPOINT_BASE,\\n        seed=args.seed,\\n        wandb_enabled=not args.dry_run,\\n        overwrite=args.dry_run,\\n        resume=args.resume,\\n    )\\n\\n    return config\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    args = parse_args()\\n\\n    logging.basicConfig(\\n        level=logging.INFO,\\n        format=\\\"%(asctime)s [%(levelname)s] %(message)s\\\",\\n    )\\n\\n    if \\\"CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES\\\" not in os.environ:\\n        logging.warning(\\\"CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES not set, will use default GPU\\\")\\n\\n    scene_cfg = SCENE_CONFIG[args.scene]\\n    logging.info(\\\"Scene: %s\\\", scene_cfg[\\\"description\\\"])\\n    logging.info(\\\"Seed: %d, Dry run: %s\\\", args.seed, args.dry_run)\\n\\n    data_dir = args.data_dir or os.path.join(DATA_BASE, scene_cfg[\\\"data_subdir\\\"])\\n    weight_path = args.weight_path or os.path.join(WEIGHTS_BASE, \\\"pi0_base\\\")\\n\\n    if not os.path.exists(data_dir):\\n        logging.error(\\\"Data directory not found: %s\\\", data_dir)\\n        sys.exit(1)\\n\\n    weight_file = os.path.join(weight_path, \\\"model.safetensors\\\")\\n    if not os.path.exists(weight_file):\\n        logging.error(\\\"Base weight file not found: %s\\\", weight_file)\\n        sys.exit(1)\\n\\n    config = build_config(args)\\n    hooks = BaselineLoRAHooks()\\n    logging.info(\\\"Checkpoint dir: %s\\\", config.checkpoint_dir)\\n    logging.info(\\\"Weight path: %s (verified)\\\", config.pytorch_weight_path)\\n    logging.info(\\\"Training steps: %d, Batch size: %d, LR: %.2e\\\",\\n                 config.num_train_steps, config.batch_size, args.lr)\\n\\n    train_loop(config, hooks=hooks)\\n\\n\\nif __name__ {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01PyNoujnoorJk147nLeDHEh","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"**重大发现！训练 config vs eval config 不匹配：**\n\n训练 (`train_baseline.py`) 使用：\n- `extra_delta_transform=False` — 模型直接学 raw delta actions\n- `repo_id=\"safelibero_object\"` — norm_stats asset_id\n\neval 默认 config `\"pi0_libero_low_mem_finetune\"` 使用：\n- `extra_delta_transform=True` — 会在 output 加 `AbsoluteActions`（错误地把 state 加到 action 上）\n- `repo_id=\"physical-intelligence/libero\"` — norm_stats 路径不匹配\n\n这会导致两个问题：1) norm_stats 加载失败 2) 即使加载了也会错误地做 AbsoluteActions。\n\n让我确认 eval v10 实际是怎么运行的，以及检查 `serve_policy.py`。"}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3309,"cache_read_input_tokens":99127,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":3309},"output_tokens":8,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a07420f3-0e6b-4faa-8dd2-832b7168b989","uuid":"52ba27b2-ec9f-4560-a4bc-b2fb5673faf8"}

The model outputs normalized deltas, which get unnormalized to actual deltas, then added to the current state (ee_pos ≈ 0.2), resulting in actions that are way too large when the environment interprets them as deltas instead of absolute positions.\n\nBut there's another issue — the checkpoint path points to `baseline_spatial_bowl_seed0/8000/`, so the norm_stats should be loaded from `8000/assets/safelibero_spatial/norm_stats.json`, yet the config expects them at `physical-intelligence/libero/norm_stats.json`.
